<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I can see the full cascade. Table 28 (appendix) has the same incorrect SC baselines:
- LLaMA: SC 72.50%, gap -17.50
- Qwen2.5-14B: SC 74.00%, gap -19.00
- Qwen3-14B: SC 85.83%, gap -30.83

And importantly, the Δ values in Table 28 are computed from these incorrect baselines:
- LLaMA w=1: Combo 73.33%, Δ = 73.33-72.50 = +0.83pp


- LLaMA w=3: Combo 73.33%, Δ = 73.33-72.50 = +0.83pp
- LLaMA w=5: Combo 72.00%, Δ = 72.00-72.50 = -0.50pp

But if the correct SC baseline is actually 70.00%, then the deltas shift significantly—LLaMA w=1 and w=3 would both show +3.33pp improvements, while w=5 would show +2.00pp, revealing that the reported improvements are actually much larger than what's currently documented.

The same pattern holds for Qwen2.5-14B with a 73.50% baseline and Qwen3-14B with an 85.00% baseline—each configuration shows notably bigger gains when recalculated against these corrected baselines, which would also affect the statistical significance calculations.

But wait, I need to reconsider what the Worker actually reported. For LLaMA, the combo accuracy values match what's in Table 28, but for Qwen3-14B, the Worker flagged that both the SC baseline and combo accuracy don't align with the data—there are discrepancies in 5 SC questions and 2 combo questions. So the issue isn't just the baseline; the combo accuracy itself might be wrong for Qwen3.

This points to a fundamental problem: I need to identify which data source is authoritative for each model. Beyond that, if both the SC baseline and the delta change, I'll need to recompute the p-values from the raw per-problem predictions rather than just recalculating from the new delta, since McNemar tests depend on the actual prediction pairs.

For LLaMA w=3, if SC drops to 70% and delta becomes +3.33pp, the raw p-value shifts to 0.0519—which is close to significance. The real question is whether applying Benjamini-Hochberg correction across all twelve conditions would let any of the LLaMA variants or non-Mistral conditions survive, potentially expanding beyond the two conditions that currently pass correction. Even with the corrected p-value, LLaMA's result wouldn't pass the Benjamini-Hochberg threshold. The original claim about generators with high self-consistency showing no improvement needs reworking since LLaMA sits at 70% self-consistency with a +3.33pp improvement, not the stated thresholds. A more defensible framing would be that generators exceeding the verifier's baseline accuracy show no significant improvement after multiple comparison correction.

Looking at the gap calculation more carefully, I see it's defined as verifier accuracy minus generator self-consistency, which explains why Mistral's gap is +15.67 (55% minus 39.33%). This actually strengthens the capability gap pattern—LLaMA's corrected gap of -15.00 still demonstrates the monotonic relationship where improvement decreases as self-consistency increases. The exact p-value for perfect correlation with n=6 is 1/720 ≈ 0.00139 for one-sided or 0.00278 for two-sided, so the paper's reported p=0.003 is a reasonable approximation. The Figure 7 statistical conclusion still holds, though I'd need to replot the data points with the corrected Gap values. I think I've analyzed this enough—let me have the Agent investigate the data and then direct the necessary fixes.

While waiting for confirmation, I should check if the Round 19 review has come back, prepare a fix plan to dispatch immediately once the Agent confirms the correct values, and review the issues in the YAML file.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01DFkhVUdN3zDD9T77KQ6VMG`</summary>

```text
0	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
1	\label{sec:analysis}
2	
3	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
4	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
5	
6	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. The $\alpha$-insensitivity confirms degeneration: under $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ the penalty term vanishes and $\alpha$ becomes irrelevant, producing $\geq$99\% agreement across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ as a ceiling effect (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that BR reflects structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
9	
```

0	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
1	\label{sec:analysis}
2	
3	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
4	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
5	
6	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on $\geq$99\% of problems across all weight settings $\alpha$ (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting.
7	
8	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that BR reflects structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
9

Very useful data! Let me extract the key findings:

1. **GPU cleanup done**: PID 681007 killed, cuda:3 freed (86 GB)

3. **Two experiments completed**: llama8b_pw_seed123 (n=600) and llama8b_pw_seed42 (n=600)

Important finding from the completed experiments:
- **LLaMA-8B PW seed42: SC=72.33%** — this is close to the paper's 72.50%!


- **LLaMA-8B PW seed123: SC=71.33%** — slightly lower but still in the ballpark

So the multi-seed SICA experiments are showing some variance across runs, with seed42 performing better. The Worker audit was checking a different experiment (the NLI combo variant), which explains why that one showed SC=70.00% — these are distinct experimental setups with different results depending on the seed and configuration.

The real methodological question is whether I should be comparing the combo experiment's accuracy against its own SC baseline (70.00%) or against the standalone SICA SC (72.33%). If the combo experiment generated its own K traces and computed SC from those, then using 70.00% as the baseline makes sense for calculating Δ. Mixing in SC from a different seed would be comparing different experimental conditions, which would skew the results. Neither exactly matches 72.50% — seed42 gives 72.33%, which is very close but not exact. This suggests the paper's result might come from a different run entirely. The real issue is that the combo experiment generates its own traces and produces SC=70.00%, which is what should be used in the paper rather than pulling from a separate run.

For Qwen2.5-14B, I'm seeing multiple different SC values depending on the experiment and seed: the standalone SICA gets 74.00%, the NLI combo gives 73.50%, and the multi-seed run with seed123 shows 72.33%. These variations stem from different K-trace samples at different seeds. For Table 3, I should use SC=73.50% since that comes from the same traces as the combo experiment.

The multi-seed results for LLaMA are starting to come in, which will help validate the Table 1 entries. But the immediate focus is getting Table 3 corrected. I'm also noticing a discrepancy with Qwen3-14B where the Worker audit found SC=85.00% but the paper reports 85.83%, and the combo shows 86.00% versus the paper's 86.33% — the d116 experiment file doesn't seem to match what's in the paper, so there might be a different experiment being referenced.

Agent will need to investigate whether the Qwen3-14B results came from a different seed or run. There's also the crashed qwen25_pw_seed456 experiment that stopped at n=478/600, which could be restarted on cuda:3 now that it's free, but that's lower priority than fixing Table 3. The queue seems properly ordered with the GPU cleanup and urgent Table 3 message next. I should check if the review submission response has arrived.

   - File: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/artifacts/nli_tiebreaking_audit.json`
   - Status: Qwen3-14B marked as "NEEDS_RECOMPUTATION" (data at westd-16639:d116_qwen3_pw_deberta_large)

   - File: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/logs/workers/worker_audit_table1-table2-data-consistency_20260523T102233_result.json`
   - Reports SC=85.83% (alphabetical tiebreaking), SICA=85.67%

   - DeBERTa-FT Combo: SC=85.83%, Combo w=1: 86.50%, w=3: 88.00%, w=5: 90.00%

### **Key SC Value Discrepancies Explaining Table 3 vs d116:**

| SC Value | Source | Method | Explanation |
|----------|--------|--------|-------------|
| **85.00%** (510/600) | d116 result file | Insertion-order tiebreaking | Original stored value with Python dict ordering |
| **85.83%** (515/600) | D061 recomputed (alphabetical) | Alphabetical tiebreaking | Recalculated using sorted(ties)[0] method |
| **85.67%** | Dir-K SICA baseline | Separate experiment | Different computation path |
| **85.83%** | DeBERTa-FT combo table | Alphabetical tiebreaking | Consistent with D061 standard |

### **Root Causes:**

1. **Tiebreaking inconsistency**: Different experiments and paper tables use different tiebreaking methods (insertion-order vs alphabetical), causing 0.83pp difference
2. **Multiple experiment runs**: Different K traces and random seeds between d116, Dir-K, and other experiments
3. **Paper inconsistency**: Table 1 uses stored 85.00%, but Table 3 cross-gen uses D061 recomputed 85.83%

This explains why Table 3 shows SC=85.83% while d116_qwen3_pw_deberta_large/results.json shows SC=85.00% — they're using different tiebreaking conventions and possibly different traces.

Excellent! This clarifies the Qwen3 discrepancy:

- **d116 result file**: SC=85.00% (insertion-order tiebreaking)
- **D061 recomputed with alphabetical tiebreaking**: SC=85.83%
- The paper uses alphabetical tiebreaking consistently across Table 1 and Table 2 (as confirmed in the Table 1/2 audit)

So the Qwen3 Table 3 value of SC=85.83% is the alphabetically-tiebroken version. This is actually consistent with the paper's tiebreaking convention.


Now I'm noticing something important: d116 shows SC=85.00% with insertion-order tiebreaking, but when I apply alphabetical tiebreaking it becomes 85.83%. The combo values (w1=85.83%, w3=86.00%, w5=86.83%) appear to follow the same pattern, but I need to verify whether these were also recomputed with alphabetical tiebreaking or if they're using a different calculation method.

Looking at the discrepancy between Table 3 in the paper (Combo w3: 86.33%) and the d116 data file (w3: 86.00%), that 0.33pp gap suggests the combo might have undergone the same tiebreaking adjustment. The real question is whether both SC and combo use the same tiebreaking approach, which would determine the correct delta values. The paper standardized on alphabetical tiebreaking, so I need to ensure SC, combo, and their delta all use the same method consistently. Within each experiment, both the SC baseline and combo results should apply alphabetical tiebreaking to the same set of traces, then compute the delta and p-value from that consistent comparison.

If the stored results used insertion-order tiebreaking instead, the agent will need to recompute everything from the raw per-problem predictions. For Qwen3, the 85.83% versus 85.00% gap appears to be a tiebreaking artifact rather than a data error, but I need to verify that combo accuracy also gets recomputed with consistent tiebreaking. The LLaMA discrepancy of 72.50% versus 70.00% is more troubling since it represents 15 problems—much larger than typical tiebreaking effects would account for.

I should check the NLI tiebreaking audit file that the Explore agent flagged as needing recomputation for Qwen3.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is extremely important! The NLI tiebreaking audit reveals that:

1. **Mistral-7B combo_w3**: Paper reports 42.83% but alphabetical tiebreaking gives **46.00%** (a 3.17pp difference!). This changes Δ from +3.50pp to +6.67pp. And p changes from 0.0125 to 1.1e-05.

Wait, looking at Table 3 in the paper: Mistral-7B combo w=3 already shows +6.67pp and p<0.001. So it seems like Table 3 was ALREADY corrected to use alphabetical tiebreaking! But the tiebreaking audit says combo_w3 = 46.00% with alpha, and SC = 39.33%. 

The difference is 46.00 - 39.33 = +6.67, which matches Table 3 exactly. So Mistral-7B already has the correct alphabetical values in the paper.

For the remaining models, I need to check whether their reported combo scores also need alphabetical tiebreak recomputation: LLaMA, Qwen2.5, and Qwen3 all have stored SC values that require recalculation, and I need to verify if their combo w3 numbers in the paper are already corrected or not.

Looking at the paper's Table 3 values against what's stored, there's a clear pattern—the paper shows higher SC scores for all three models compared to what's in storage, suggesting the alphabetical tiebreak was applied to SC but I need to determine if it was also applied to the combo scores. alpha tiebreak (estimated):
- LLaMA: SC might increase slightly (from 70.00% to maybe 70.5-71%), combo might change too
- Qwen2.5: SC might change from 73.50% to maybe 74.00% (paper's value?)
- Qwen3: SC changes from 85.00% to 85.83% (confirmed by D061 recomputation)

So the picture is becoming clearer:
- The paper has some values that are alpha-tiebroken (Qwen3 SC=85.83%, Mistral combo) and some that are not or from different sources (LLaMA SC=72.50%)
- The inconsistency is that SC and combo need to be computed from the same traces with the same tiebreaking

The critical issue is LLaMA: even with alpha tiebreaking, the jump from 70.00% to 72.50% is too large — that's 15 additional problems correct, far more than tiebreaking could explain. So LLaMA's 72.50% must come from a completely different trace set than the combo experiment's 70.00%.

The methodologically sound approach is to use the combo experiment's traces, but I need to verify whether the combo experiment's SC was computed from all K traces or a subset, and whether it used a different K value entirely. Once I confirm those details, I can move forward with the right trace set.

In the meantime, let me check another flagged issue: the claim that "94% of errors overlap with SC" for Mistral-7B FOLIO. If SC produces 90 errors and SICA has a similar count, then 94% overlap would mean roughly 85 errors are shared between them.

This aligns with the >90% argmax agreement we've already established, so while the Worker couldn't independently verify the error set comparison, it's consistent enough that I won't prioritize it. Instead, I should focus on the concrete fix plan: extracting the correct SC and combo values with proper alpha tiebreaking from the experiment traces, computing the delta, and recalculating the McNemar p-values once Agent confirms the values.

Now I need to update the tables and figures systematically—Table 3 and Table 28, revise the text in solutions.tex to use a more robust phrasing around the significance claims that doesn't lock in specific thresholds, check whether Figure 7's Gap values shift, apply BH correction, and regenerate the figure if needed. I'll reframe the claim to say something like "all generators above the verifier show no significant gain after BH correction" with the p-value range rather than exact deltas. I'm ready to move forward once Agent provides confirmation.

核心结论：Agent 需要从 combo 实验的原始 per-problem 预测出发，用统一的 alphabetical tiebreaking 重算所有值。Mistral 已正确，其他三个需要重算。

```text
Let me map out all occurrences that need changing:

**LLaMA SC=72.50% (Table 3 context, ProofWriter):**
1. solutions.tex:27 — Table 3 row
2. appendix.tex:960 — Table 28 header
3. appendix.tex:996 — Finetuned NLI table header  
4. figures/gen_capability_gap_scatter.py:49 — Figure generation script

**Qwen2.5-14B SC=74.00% (Table 3 context, ProofWriter):**


1. solutions.tex:28 — Table 3 row
2. appendix.tex:965 — Table 28 header

But I need to be careful here: experiments.tex:31 also shows Qwen2.5-14B PW-D5 SC=74.00%, but that's from the standalone SICA experiment (Table 1), not the NLI combo experiment. The 74.00% in experiments.tex and diagnosis.tex comes from a different trace set and was already verified as correct in the Table 1/2 audit. Only the values in solutions.tex and appendix.tex need updating since those are from Table 3.

For Qwen3-14B, I'm seeing SC=85.83% in both Table 1 and Table 3, but the underlying combo experiment stored 85.00% before alpha tiebreaking. After applying alpha tiebreak, it should become 85.83%, which matches what's in Table 3. So Table 3 might actually be correct—it just needs the combo accuracy to also be alpha-tiebroken. But the Worker audit found something that contradicts this.

Looking at the d116 results: the SC value goes from 85.00% to 85.83% after alpha tiebreak, which checks out. The combo_w3 value is 86.00% and needs recomputation after alpha tiebreaking. Paper Table 3 shows w3 as 86.33%, so if alpha tiebreaking changes combo_w3 from 86.00% to 86.33%, that's only about 2 problems different—plausible for tiebreaking effects.

So the pattern emerging is: Qwen3's SC is correct at 85.83% after alpha tiebreak, but the combo values need recomputation to verify. Qwen2.5's SC also needs alpha tiebreak recomputation since it might not actually be 74.00%. LLaMA's SC is sitting at 70.00% and needs similar verification.

For LLaMA specifically, the gap between 70.00% and the 72.50% in the paper is too large for tiebreaking alone to explain—that must come from a different trace set. The methodologically correct approach is to use the combo experiment's traces for both SC and combo measurements so Δ compares them on the same data.

I've identified all the affected locations and I'm waiting for the server-side recomputation to confirm the values. In the meantime, I've already found and started fixing the data integrity issues the user asked about, including the Table 2 problems.

Now I'm checking what else needs attention—there are several cascading updates tied to the LLaMA/Qwen SC baselines that Agent is currently investigating, plus I need to see if the Round 19 review has progressed.

_source line 3330 · 2026-05-23T11:08:50.429Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012axCttzjTKgpWiYcDFHpjB`</summary>

```text
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). These results point to a \emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though with $n{=}6$ this should be interpreted cautiously. Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
```

935	
936	Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli} reports the controlled cross-generator comparison with DeBERTa-large-mnli on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$). We extend this analysis by pairing Mistral-7B with two additional NLI architectures: BART-large-mnli (46.17\% standalone, combo $+5.17$\,pp, $p = 0.0002$) and RoBERTa-large-mnli (37.67\% standalone, combo $+4.17$\,pp, $p = 0.003$). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains on Mistral-7B at $w \geq 3$.
937	
938	Across all six ProofWriter verifier-generator pairs at $w{=}3$ (three verifiers $\times$ Mistral-7B plus three negative-gap generators $\times$ DeBERTa), combo gain decreases monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n = 6$; however, four of six conditions share the DeBERTa verifier, which may reduce effective degrees of freedom---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Significant gains are observed only for Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), two of twelve conditions survive: Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.42 \times 10^{-6}$).
939

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Fkked7A9EhrcLBF7RyysjH`</summary>

```text
1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2	
```

3	
4	\looseness=-1 We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the bias: zero-shot gains for the weakest generator ($p < 0.001$); domain adaptation extends to generators with SC up to 72\%. DRC recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% cost.

0	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
1	\label{sec:analysis}
2	
3	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
4	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
5	
6	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on $\geq$99\% of problems across all weight settings $\alpha$ (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting.
7	
8	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ across all tested conditions ($p > 0.7$), confirming that BR reflects structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
9	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NzwugQa2QvVwYt3Gs5BKKV`</summary>

```text
44	\smallskip\noindent
45	\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
46	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
47	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace within each answer group, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
48	
49	\smallskip\noindent
50	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
51	Under degeneration, $\mathcal{C}^* \approx \mathcal{C}$ and the penalty term vanishes; source-attribution restricts each constraint's contribution to its originating answer; approximately uniform yield makes total weight proportional to $n_a$, preserving the argmax. SICA and majority vote select the same answer on over 90\% of problems across all tested conditions.
52	
53	\smallskip\noindent
54	\textbf{Remark 1} \textit{(Information Ceiling).}\;%
55	If constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K};\, Y) \leq I(T_{1:K};\, Y)$---a standard result for any deterministic post-processing. The contribution is identifying the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain structure: under the degeneration condition of Observation~1 ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), constraints are fully determined by answers, yielding the tighter bound $I(C;\, Y) \leq I(A;\, Y)$ and the approximate conditional independence $C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$ shown in Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}. This is an empirical conclusion holding under degeneration, not an unconditional structural claim.
```

33	
34	\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}
35	\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\% standalone) amplifies the escape: combo gains reach $+$12.67\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+$11.67\,pp (LLaMA-8B), a $14\times$ amplification over zero-shot, with near-total error complementarity (Table~\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}, Appendix). Domain-adapted verification extends escape to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\%, consistent with the prediction that the capability gap can be shifted by increasing verifier accuracy. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}35\%$ NLI cost (Appendix~\ref{app:drc}). The domain-adapted verifier introduces training, so these results serve as directional evidence for the capability-gap pattern rather than a proposed method.
36

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01BM2WeZQ1QXLzBzkadGdmSk`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all three generators above the verifier show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement after BH correction ($\Delta \leq +3.33$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly at $w{=}3$ ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); the three above-verifier generators show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement after BH correction ($\Delta \leq +3.33$\,pp), though LLaMA-3.1-8B reaches significance at $w{=}1$ ($p_{\text{BH}} < 0.005$; Table~\ref{tab:nli-combo-full}). These results point to a \emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though with $n{=}6$ this should be interpreted cautiously. Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability. Only Mistral-7B (SC below the verifier) shows a significant gain.}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 70.00\% & $-$15.00 & $+$3.33\,pp & 0.052 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 73.50\% & $-$18.50 & $+$1.17\,pp & 0.230 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.00\% & $-$30.00 & $+$1.00\,pp & 0.146 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}
35	\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\% standalone) amplifies the escape: combo gains reach $+$12.67\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+$11.67\,pp (LLaMA-8B), a $14\times$ amplification over zero-shot, with near-total error complementarity (Table~\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}, Appendix). Domain-adapted verification extends escape to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\%, consistent with the prediction that the capability gap can be shifted by increasing verifier accuracy. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}35\%$ NLI cost (Appendix~\ref{app:drc}). The domain-adapted verifier introduces training, so these results serve as directional evidence for the capability-gap pattern rather than a proposed method.
36

      "content": "\\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Motivation.}\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \\leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all three generators above the verifier show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement after BH correction ($\\Delta \\leq +3.33$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly at $w{=}3$ ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); the three above-verifier generators show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement after BH correction ($\\Delta \\leq +3.33$\\,pp), though LLaMA-3.1-8B reaches significance at $w{=}1$ ($p_{\\text{BH}} < 0.005$; Table~\\ref{tab:nli-combo-full}). These results point to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. 935	
936	Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli} reports the controlled cross-generator comparison with DeBERTa-large-mnli on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$). We extend this analysis by pairing Mistral-7B with two additional NLI architectures: BART-large-mnli (46.17\% standalone, combo $+5.17$\,pp, $p = 0.0002$) and RoBERTa-large-mnli (37.67\% standalone, combo $+4.17$\,pp, $p = 0.003$). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains on Mistral-7B at $w \geq 3$.
937	
938	Across all six ProofWriter verifier-generator pairs at $w{=}3$ (three verifiers $\times$ Mistral-7B plus three negative-gap generators $\times$ DeBERTa), combo gain decreases monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n = 6$; however, four of six conditions share the DeBERTa verifier, which may reduce effective degrees of freedom---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Significant $w{=}3$ gains are observed only for Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier); the three above-verifier generators show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), three of twelve conditions survive: Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$), $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.42 \times 10^{-6}$), and LLaMA-3.1-8B $w{=}1$ (adj.\ $p = 4.80 \times 10^{-3}$).
939	
940	\paragraph{Robustness check.} Including all nine conditions (three verifiers $\times$ three weight levels on Mistral-7B) yields Spearman $\rho = 0.836$ (exact permutation $p = 0.007$, $n = 9$), confirming that the monotonic trend is robust to the choice of weight parameter.
941	
942	\paragraph{Full NLI combo with BH correction.}
943	\label{app:nli-combo-full}
944	
945	\begin{table}[h]

All conditions use DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone). Two of twelve conditions survive correction (Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$). {***}\,$p_{\text{BH}} < 0.001$.}

    "snippet": "1\t\\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}\n2\t\\label{sec:solutions}\n3\t\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n4\t\n5\t\\paragraph{Motivation.}\n6\t\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \\leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.\n7\t\n8\t\\paragraph{Method.}\n9\t\\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n10\t\n11\t\\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}\n12\t\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n13\t\n14\t\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n15\t\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:nli-combo-full}). These results point to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though with $n{=}6$ this should be interpreted cautiously. Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n16\t\n17\t\\begin{table}[t]\n18\t\\centering\n19\t\\small\n20\t\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability. Only Mistral-7B (SC below the verifier) shows a significant $w{=}3$ gain.}\n21\t\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n22\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n23\t\\toprule\n24\tGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n25\t\\midrule\n26\tMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\n27\tLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\n28\tQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\n29\tQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n30\t\\bottomrule\n31\t\\end{tabular}}\n32\t\\end{table}\n33\t\n34\t\\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}\n35\t\\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\\% standalone) amplifies the escape: combo gains reach $+$12.67\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+$11.67\\,pp (LLaMA-8B), a $14\\times$ amplification over zero-shot, with near-total error complementarity (Table~\\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}, Appendix). Domain-adapted verification extends escape to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\%, consistent with the prediction that the capability gap can be shifted by increasing verifier accuracy. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}35\\%$ NLI cost (Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}). The domain-adapted verifier introduces training, so these results serve as directional evidence for the capability-gap pattern rather than a proposed method."

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **SICA**: Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation — extracts FOL constraints from CoT traces, selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT (Z3)
   - **Symbolic Confirmation Bias (SCB)**: Self-extracted constraints systematically support model's majority answer
   - **Observation 1 (Argmax Preservation)**: Under degeneration, source-attribution scoring, and uniform constraint yield, SICA reduces to majority vote
   - **NLI Combo**: Cross-architecture verification using DeBERTa NLI verifier paired with LLM generators
   - **Capability Gap Pattern**: Verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Spearman ρ=1.0, n=6)
   - **Alphabetical Tiebreaking**: Paper standard for resolving majority-vote ties; NLI combo scripts used insertion-order (Counter.most_common()) — a bug
   - **BH Correction**: Benjamini-Hochberg FDR control across m=12 NLI combo conditions
   - **SC Baseline Mismatch**: Root cause of Table 3 errors — LLaMA's 72.50% came from finetuned DeBERTa combo experiment traces, wrongly used in zero-shot Table 3

        "content": "\\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Motivation.}\n\\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \\leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\\geq 72\\%$ show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp). Same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:nli-combo-full}). These results point to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though with $n{=}6$ this should be interpreted cautiously. Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability. Only Mistral-7B (SC below the verifier) shows a significant $w{=}3$ gain.}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.665 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.503 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.453 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}\n\\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\\% standalone) amplifies the escape: combo gains reach $+$12.67\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+$11.67\\,pp (LLaMA-8B), a $14\\times$ amplification over zero-shot, with near-total error complementarity (Table~\\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}, Appendix). Domain-adapted verification extends escape to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\%, consistent with the prediction that the capability gap can be shifted by increasing verifier accuracy. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}35\\%$ NLI cost (Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}). The domain-adapted verifier introduces training, so these results serve as directional evidence for the capability-gap pattern rather than a proposed method.\n",

        "content": "{\n  \"raw\": \"修复完成。总结：\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 已修复\\n\\n**Task 1 — SC Baseline**:\\n- 论文的错误 SC 值来自 finetuned 实验（72.50%），被误用到 zero-shot Table 3\\n- 正确值：LLaMA 70.00%、Qwen2.5 73.50%、Qwen3 85.00%（均来自各自 combo 结果文件的 `sc_baseline` 字段）\\n- 修正了 solutions.tex Table 3、appendix.tex Table 28 的全部 SC/Gap/Δ/p/BH 值\\n\\n**Task 2 — 94.4%**:\\n- diagnosis.tex 中 \\\"94.4%\\\" 无法追溯到任何结果文件，可能是早期计算残留\\n- 已改为引用 tab:ablation 的实际数据（\\\">96%\\\"）\\n- analysis.tex 此前已独立修正为 \\\"≥99%\\\"（引用 tab:alpha-ablation），无需改动\\n\\n**Task 3 — 文件修改**:\\n- `solutions.tex`: Table 3 数据 + 两处正文引用\\n- `appendix.tex`: Table 28 全表 + 叙述文字 + caption\\n- `diagnosis.tex`: 94.4% → >96%\\n- `figures/gen_capability_gap_scatter.py`: 3 个数据点坐标\\n\\n**Task 4 — 验证**:\\n- grep 确认无旧值残留（72.50% 仅存于 finetuned 表和注释中，均正确）\\n- 修复报告已写入 `artifacts/table3_fix_report.md` 并注册\\n\\n### 需 Director 决策的级联影响\\n\\n1. **Figure 7 需重新生成**（脚本已更新，PDF 需重跑）\\n2. **LLaMA w=1 现在 BH 显著**（p_BH=0.0048），原论文 \\\"only Mistral gains significantly\\\" 的叙事需要调整——显著条件从 2→3 个\\n3. **Qwen3 SC=85.83% 在 experiments.tex/diagnosis.tex 的 SICA 主表中可能也有误**（exp033 数据显示 sc=85.00%），建议单独核查\\n4. **Spearman ρ=1.0** 排序仍单调递减（6.67>3.33>1.17>1.00），但具体统计值建议用新数据重算\"\n}",

{

        "content": "# Table 3 数据修复 + 94.4% 引用修复报告\n\n## 1. SC Baseline 调查结果\n\n### 数据来源确认\n\n每个 generator 的 NLI combo 实验结果文件（westd-16639）：\n\n| Generator | 结果文件 | 文件中 SC | 论文 SC | 差异 |\n|-----------|---------|----------|--------|------|\n| Mistral-7B | `r11_3verifier_canonical.json` | 39.33% (236/600) | 39.33% | ✅ 一致 |\n| LLaMA-3.1-8B | `exp092_llama8b_pw_nli_combo/results.json` | 70.00% (420/600) | 72.50% | ❌ -2.50pp |\n| Qwen2.5-14B | `qwen14b_pw_nli_combo/results.json` | 73.50% (441/600) | 74.00% | ❌ -0.50pp |\n| Qwen3-14B | `d116_qwen3_pw_deberta_large/results.json` | 85.00% (510/600) | 85.83% | ❌ -0.83pp |\n\n### 72.50% 的来源\n\n`artifacts/nli_recompute_exp091_exp092.json`（2026-05-22 tiebreaking 修复记录）确认：\n- combo 实验的 SC baseline 始终是 70.00%（LLaMA）和 73.50%（Qwen2.5）\n- 72.50% 来自 **finetuned DeBERTa combo 实验**（`deberta_ft_combo_all_fixed/results.json`，`llama8b.sc_baseline = 0.725`），是不同实验的不同 traces，被错误地用到了 zero-shot Table 3\n\n### Multi-seed 验证\n\nLLaMA-3.1-8B multi-seed SICA 实验的 SC 值：\n- seed42: 72.20% (n=223, 不完整)\n- seed123: 71.33% (n=600)\n- seed456: 73.55% (n=121, 不完整)\n- seed789: 74.30% (n=323, 不完整)\n\n均不是 72.50%。combo 实验（n=600, SC=70.00%）是 Table 3 的唯一正确来源。\n\n---\n\n## 2. Table 3 修正值\n\n### 修正后的 Table 3 (tab:cross_generator_nli, w=3)\n\n| Generator | Family | SC | Gap | Combo Δ (w=3) | p |\n|-----------|--------|------|------|-------------|---|\n| Mistral-7B | Mistral | 39.33% | +15.67 | +6.67pp | <0.001 |\n| **LLaMA-3.1-8B** | LLaMA | **70.00%** | **-15.00** | **+3.33pp** | **0.052** |\n| **Qwen2.5-14B** | Qwen | **73.50%** | **-18.50** | **+1.17pp** | **0.230** |\n| **Qwen3-14B** | Qwen | **85.00%** | **-30.00** | **+1.00pp** | **0.146** |\n\n### 数据溯源\n\n- **LLaMA SC=70.00%**: `exp092_llama8b_pw_nli_combo/results.json` → `sc_baseline: 0.7`\n- **LLaMA w3 Δ=+3.33pp**: 同文件 → `combo.w3.delta_pp: 3.33`\n- **LLaMA w3 p=0.052**: 同文件 → `combo.w3.mcnemar_p: 0.0519`（四舍五入）\n- **Qwen2.5 SC=73.50%**: `qwen14b_pw_nli_combo/results.json` → `sc_baseline: 0.735`\n- **Qwen2.5 w3 Δ=+1.17pp**: 同文件 → `combo.w3.delta_pp: 1.17`\n- **Qwen2.5 w3 p=0.230**: 同文件 → `combo.w3.mcnemar_p: 0.2295`\n- **Qwen3 SC=85.00%**: `d116_qwen3_pw_deberta_large/results.json` → `sc_baseline: 0.85`\n- **Qwen3 w3 Δ=+1.00pp**: 同文件 → `combo.w3.delta_pp: 1.0`\n- **Qwen3 w3 p=0.146**: 同文件 → `combo.w3.mcnemar_p: 0.146`\n\n---\n\n## 3. BH 校正重算\n\n### 新的 12 条件 BH 校正（m=12, α=0.05）\n\n| Rank | Condition | Raw p | BH-adjusted p | Significant? |\n|------|-----------|-------|---------------|-------------|\n| 1 | Mistral w5 | 6.18e-7 | 7.42e-6 | *** |\n| 2 | Mistral w3 | 1.13e-5 | 6.78e-5 | *** |\n| 3 | **LLaMA w1** | **0.0012** | **0.0048** | *** → **新增** ** |\n| 4 | LLaMA w3 | 0.0519 | 0.125 | |\n| 5 | Qwen3 w5 | 0.0522 | 0.125 | |\n| 6 | Qwen3 w1 | 0.125 | 0.250 | |\n| 7 | Qwen3 w3 | 0.146 | 0.250 | |\n| 8 | Qwen2.5 w5 | 0.1934 | 0.290 | |\n| 9 | Qwen2.5 w3 | 0.2295 | 0.306 | |\n| 10 | Qwen2.5 w1 | 0.3438 | 0.385 | |\n| 11 | Mistral w1 | 0.359 | 0.385 | |\n| 12 | LLaMA w5 | 0.3846 | 0.385 | |\n\n**关键变化**: 显著条件从 2 个变为 3 个。LLaMA-3.1-8B w=1（+3.33pp, p_BH=0.0048）在 BH 校正后显著。\n\n---\n\n## 4. 94.4% 调查结果\n\n### 来源\n\ndiagnosis.tex:118 原文：\n> \"constraint ablation shows MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4% of predictions (n=804)\"\n\n### 数据检查\n\n- `tab:ablation`（appendix.tex:385-400）显示：\n  - SICA (MAX-SAT) vs SC agreement: 773/804 = **96.14%**\n  - Count-only vs SC agreement: 774/804 = **96.27%**\n- grep 所有结果文件，未找到 94.4% 作为 agreement 指标\n- `0.944` 出现在日志中均为单个问题的 `satisfied_ratio`（如 34/36=0.944），与全局 agreement 无关\n- analysis.tex 已更新为 \"≥99%\"（引用 tab:alpha-ablation，FOLIO n=204 数据，正确）\n\n### 结论\n\n94.4% 无法追溯到任何结果文件，可能来自早期版本计算。已修正为引用 tab:ablation 的实际数据（>96%）。\n\n---\n\n## 5. 已执行的修改\n\n### 5.1 solutions.tex\n\n| 位置 | 修改内容 |\n|------|---------|\n| Line 12 | \"SC ≥ 72%\" → \"all three generators above the verifier\"; \"Δ ≤ +0.83pp\" → \"Δ ≤ +3.33pp\" |\n| Line 15 | \"47-point\" → \"46-point\"; \"Δ ≤ +0.83pp\" → \"Δ ≤ +3.33pp\"; 添加 LLaMA w=1 显著性说明 |\n| Line 27 | LLaMA: SC 72.50→70.00, Gap -17.50→-15.00, Δ +0.83→+3.33, p 0.665→0.052 |\n| Line 28 | Qwen2.5: SC 74.00→73.50, Gap -19.00→-18.50, Δ +0.67→+1.17, p 0.503→0.230 |\n| Line 29 | Qwen3: SC 85.83→85.00, Gap -30.83→-30.00, Δ +0.50→+1.00, p 0.45→0.146 |\n\n### 5.2 appendix.tex\n\n| 位置 | 修改内容 |\n|------|---------|\n| Line 938 | \"SC ≥ 72%\" → \"above-verifier generators\"; \"two of twelve\" → \"three of twelve\"; 添加 LLaMA w=1 |\n| Line 948 (caption) | \"Two of twelve\" → \"Three of twelve\"; 添加 {**} 标注 |\n| Lines 955-973 | Table 28 全部数据更新：SC、Gap、Δ、p_raw、p_BH、significance markers |\n\n### 5.3 diagnosis.tex\n\n| 位置 | 修改内容 |\n|------|---------|\n| Line 118 | \"MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4%\" → \"both MAX-SAT and count-only each agree with SC on >96% (Table ref)\" |\n\n### 5.4 figures/gen_capability_gap_scatter.py\n\n| 位置 | 修改内容 |\n|------|---------|\n| Line 32 | Qwen3 PW-D5: (-30.83, 0.50) → (-30.00, 1.00) |\n| Line 33 | Qwen2.5 PW-D5: (-19.00, 0.67) → (-18.50, 1.17) |\n| Line 39 | LLaMA: (-17.50, 0.83) → (-15.00, 3.33) |\n| Annotations | 对应坐标更新 |\n\n---\n\n## 6. 级联影响（需 Director 决策）\n\n### 6.1 Figure 7 (capability_gap_scatter) 需要重新生成\n\nfigure 脚本已更新，但 PDF 需要在服务器上重新运行 Python 生成。LLaMA 数据点从 (gap=-17.5, Δ=0.83) 移动到 (gap=-15.0, Δ=3.33)，图形外观会有明显变化。\n\n### 6.2 叙事影响（CRITICAL）\n\n修正后的数据改变了论文的一个核心论点：\n\n**旧叙事**: \"只有 Mistral（SC 低于 verifier）获得显著提升；所有 SC ≥ 72% 的 generator 无显著提升（Δ ≤ 0.83pp）\" — 干净的 capability gap 二分法。\n\n**新叙事**: LLaMA-3.1-8B（SC=70%, 高于 verifier 的 55%）在 w=1 时获得 **BH 校正后显著** 的 +3.33pp（p_BH=0.0048）。这意味着：\n1. Capability gap 不是简单的 \"低于 verifier 才有效\"\n2. 即使 SC 高于 verifier 15pp 的 generator 也能从外部验证中获益\n3. 不过 w=3 时 LLaMA 的 p=0.052 未通过 BH，且 gain 随 SC 增加仍单调递减\n\n**建议**: capability-gap pattern 仍然成立（monotonically decreasing），但 breakeven point 的描述需要调整。当前文本已添加 LLaMA w=1 显著性的注释，Director 可能需要进一步调整叙事框架。\n\n### 6.3 Qwen3-14B SC 在 experiments.tex 和 diagnosis.tex 中可能也有误\n\nexperiments.tex:37 和 diagnosis.tex:34 的主 SICA 结果表显示 Qwen3 SC=85.83%，但 exp033（SICA 实验）的 `sc_accuracy=0.85`（85.00%）。这是 Table 3 之外的另一个潜在不一致，建议 Director 单独核查。\n\n### 6.4 Finetuned table (tab:finetuned-nli) Mistral SC 微小差异\n\nfinetuned combo 数据文件显示 Mistral `sc_baseline=0.3917`（39.17%），但表中写 39.33%。差异 0.17pp（1 个问题），可能来自 tiebreaking 差异。建议 Director 核查是否需要修正。\n\n### 6.5 Spearman 相关性可能需要重算\n\nsolutions.tex:15 引用 \"Spearman ρ=1.0, p=0.003, n=6\"。数据点变化后，排序可能仍然完美单调（monotonically decreasing verified: 6.67 > 3.33 > 1.17 > 1.00），但 Spearman 统计量应基于新数据重新验证。\n",

        "totalLines": 171
